FOUND AND FIXED A SHARED DEFECT: parseQuery treated limit and cursor as FILTER members, and since a cursor is bound to filterHashOf(parsed.filter) while a continuation carries cursor in its raw query, page 2 hashed a different filter and EVERY continuation failed with 400 INVALID_CURSOR - so HTTP pagination was IMPOSSIBLE for every collection route (5.1, 5.3, 5.4, 5.11, 5.16 and every future one). Unnoticed because the many-page suites drive the cursor helpers directly and each route was only ever fetched one page at a time; it surfaced the first time a suite paginated through a route (5.15's walk, limit=1 over five events sharing one instant). 2026-09-15T07:14:58Z | forge | EP-004 | MILESTONE_NOTE | M6 5.10/5.11 read group complete: 4 routes (35 -> 39 of 78), migration 0018 additive. Four findings. (1) verification_observation.finding's column CHECK is PRESENT|ABSENT|INCONCLUSIVE while 5.10.2's wire tokens are RECORD_PRESENT|RECORD_ABSENT|INDETERMINATE; my fixtures wrote the wire tokens into the column and the constraint refused them, which is how the mismatch surfaced - now a frozen FINDING_TO_API table with toApiFinding() that THROWS on an unmapped value rather than defaulting to INDETERMINATE, because 'no record' is the one answer an operator must never receive by accident. (2) REAPPEARANCES_QUERY had drifted from 5.11.2 (wrong filter fields, observedAt missing from sortFields) and no test could fail because the route had no handler; found by diffing the declaration against the spec body. (3) A KEYSET CURSOR REPEATED THE ROW THAT MINTED IT: pg returns timestamptz as a JS Date (ms) while PostgreSQL stores microseconds, so a cursor minted from ..00.123456 carried ..00.123Z and page 2's predicate observed_at > '..00.123' was TRUE for that same row; at another boundary the same mismatch SKIPS a row. Fixed by date_trunc('milliseconds', ...) on BOTH the comparison and the ORDER BY in observations.ts; the IDENTICAL defect was present in subjects.ts (5.1 location history) and is fixed in the same commit. It survived every earlier suite structurally: the existing pagination tests drive the cursor helpers over synthetic millisecond-aligned rows, so truncation was a no-op in all of them; the new walk asserts each row is seen EXACTLY ONCE. audit-queries.ts (at is written from integer ms) and sources.ts (orders on text name) were checked and NOT changed, recorded as an open hazard rather than a proof. (4) The coverage number now comes from the declared node scripts/route-coverage.ts; an ad-hoc regex recount I ran first said 26 and was wrong because audit.ts declares its template array on one line. ASSUMPTIONS.md itself carried two headings numbered 3.19 - nothing references either, so the later is now 3.19-bis with the collision stated in place. Verified: unit 555/555, test-integration 187/187 twice, all static gates ok.

FIVE DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED: (1) the audit sink refused the service's OWN correlation ids - installCorrelation mints a dash-less 32-hex trace id while the column is uuid, so the FIRST write route to append an audit row answered 500 and every effect-bearing route would have; the two forms are the same 128 bits, so the sink now stores the canonical rendering and still refuses any other shape, with a positive test that the stored value joins back. (2) the seeded confidence_basis was an array of bare strings while 5.5.1's basis is {feature,weight} objects - schema-valid, contract-unrenderable, and the 5.5.1 list answered 500 for anything holding it; the reader REFUSES rather than inventing a weight (pinned by a test) and the fixture now uses the contract's shape. (4) 0020 then forbade the project's own seed (NOT NULL without DEFAULT) - fixed in 0021, since an applied migration is immutable. (5) the state file C:/tmp/vanishgraph-db.env grew to 2MB and every gate sourcing it died with Argument list too long; provision.ts's JSON-escaping is correct for a POSIX dot-source and the corruption came from my own loader re-injecting the raw double-quoted value, which the next provisioning round trip doubled again - restored, and the loader now unescapes. Also fixed: my first version of the new suite built worlds in the SEEDED tenant A, breaking three other suites' 'tenant A has exactly one seeded subject' assertions (five failures) - it now owns two tenants per run. 2026-09-15T09:08:04Z | forge | EP-004 | MILESTONE_NOTE | M6 5.7 case group complete: 6 routes (5.7.1-5.7.6), coverage 44->50 registered / 39->45 working of 78. Migration 0022 adds request_case.recipe_id, the human_gate table (SPEC-001 names HumanGate as a canonical entity; no spec gives it a field list, so the columns are exactly what 5.7.5 requires) and a PARTIAL UNIQUE INDEX making 'one live case per subject x source x exposure' a database rule. Migration 0023 resolves a CONTRADICTION: 5.7.1 names a policyDecisionId while it CREATES the case, but policy_decision.case_id was NOT NULL, so a decision offered at creation could only belong to a case that does not exist yet. I dropped that NOT NULL (and recreated the FK DEFERRABLE) because it changes the meaning of no stored value, whereas the alternative - accepting a decision belonging to a DIFFERENT case - would pair a case and a decision the policy engine never resolved together. FOUND AND FIXED A SHARED PAGINATION DEFECT OF THE SAME CLASS AS THE 5.15 ONE: a time-filterable collection could not be paginated at all, because the optional default window (last 30 days) is resolved against the clock, so page 1 and page 2 hashed different filters and EVERY continuation failed 400 INVALID_CURSOR - for 5.5.1, 5.7.2, 5.11.2 and 5.4.2. Invisible because every existing walk test passes an explicit from/to, which is the one case where the default never moves. Fixed by carrying the window IN the cursor (peekCursor + parseQuery inheritance + minted timeRange), so a walk keeps the window it started with. THE FIX'S FIRST VERSION BROKE PAGE ONE: two RFC 3339 strings pushed a real cursor past MAX_CURSOR_BYTES, encodeCursor refused to emit it, and page 1 answered 400 INVALID_CURSOR with no cursor in the request. Now epoch millis + bound 1024, with a test asserting a minted cursor fits the decoder's bound. Also: 5.7.4's guardsEvaluated.recipeSigned GATES the transition and is a real Ed25519 verification, so a deployment with no key (ADR-006 OPEN) cannot prepare a request - proven both ways with a real key pair. Two declared implementation choices recorded in ASSUMPTIONS 3.29: human-gate queue/SLA (no specification source, stored on the row) and the case-creation audit action (no domain command creates a RequestCase). Verified: unit 582/582, test-integration 217/217 TWICE, all static gates ok, rls coverage ok with the new table.

Coverage 73 registered / 69 working of 78: 4 unconditional refusals (5.1.7 and the three 5.12 dependency routes) and 5 routes not implemented, ALL of them 5.4. THE SECURITY FINDING: THE EvidenceStore PORT CANNOT ISOLATE TENANTS. Its declaration is put(content, digest) / get(digest) / verify(digest) - content-addressed by DIGEST ALONE with no tenant parameter anywhere. Two tenants uploading identical bytes produce the SAME digest, so a digest-keyed store cannot distinguish their objects: a get for one tenant would return the other's document and nothing in the port would notice. VG-TENANT-002 requires isolation to hold INDEPENDENTLY at each layer, and RLS cannot cover this one because the bytes are not in the database. Implementing the port as declared would put evidence behind a cross-tenant read no policy polices, which is why 5.12.3 refuses rather than shipping a store that 'works'; the fix is a domain change (a tenant on every operation, or a tenant-namespaced digest) recorded for the node that owns the KMS/evidence story. 5.12.1 needs TWO things that do not exist: a multipart parser (Fastify 5 does not parse multipart without a plugin, and hand-rolling a boundary parser for identity documents is not something to improvise) and an EvidenceStore. THE redaction_state CONFLICT IS NOW VISIBLE RATHER THAN HIDDEN: the column CHECK admits NONE|SCRUBBED|DENIED while 5.12.1 declares UNREDACTED|DLP_SCRUBBED, and the read route reports the STORED token verbatim - a translation table would have hidden the disagreement and chosen a vocabulary on the specification's behalf; the consequence is stated plainly and asserted by a test so it cannot be 'fixed' silently later. sizeBytes and the media type are declared by the contract and recorded nowhere, so both are reported as null rather than derived from the digest or the storage reference. requirementIds IS AN EMPTY LIST BECAUSE NO SOURCE EXISTS: VG-EVIDENCE-002's chain is requirement -> case -> artifact -> digest, the artifact carries its case and transitionIds is a containment test on audit_event.evidence_artifact_ids (the array the transition writer fills), but NOTHING maps an artifact to a requirement id - no table, no column, and REQUIREMENT_TRACEABILITY.csv is verification-side data, so filling it from a file outside the running system would make a reviewer believe a chain was verified that was not. Ordering decided deliberately: 5.12.4 resolves the ARTIFACT FIRST (an unknown artifact is 404, not a dependency error) and its refusal says why the shortcut is wrong - reporting the STORED digest as 'recomputed' would be a verification that verified nothing. The read routes report immutable: true on the strength of an absence the contract suite now asserts (no PATCH/PUT/DELETE row on any artifact path). The suite's own fixtures are FIXTURES: nothing in the running system creates an evidence artifact yet. Findings in ASSUMPTIONS 3.38. THE UI DECLARES ITS OWN WIRE TYPES because import-boundary rule 4 forbids a browser bundle from importing the server's types, and a hand-declared copy is exactly where a field name gets invented - so the suite requires every declared field name to appear in SPEC-003, and states its own limit: the check proves a name is SOMEWHERE in the contract, not that it is in the right section, which is why every declaration carries its section number and the mappers are asserted case by case. THREE VALUES THE UI REFUSES TO INVENT, asserted with tests: the coverage denominator is coverage.sourcesDeclared (SPEC-003 5.4.3 declares no sourcesTotal), the catalogue version is null because 5.4.3 declares no catalogue field at all while SPEC-004 3 requires one beside every coverage figure, and the confidence threshold is null on a list row because the threshold appears only in 5.5.3's assessment response as policyThresholdApplied. WHAT THE CLIENT REFUSES: a POST without a non-empty Idempotency-Key throws before anything is sent (VG-ACTION-001 makes at-most-once the default, and a key generated inside the client would be a NEW key on every retry, which is the same as no key), a non-2xx WITHOUT the 8.1 envelope is a TransportFailure rather than an ApiError carrying invented identifiers, and no Authorization header is fabricated when no token provider is configured - requests go out unauthenticated and the API's 401 becomes the access-denied state. There is no fixture mode in the production client (DOD-020): on a machine with no API the surfaces render the error state, which is TRUE of that machine. THE COPY GATE FOUND THREE REAL IDENTIFIER DEFECTS IN MY OWN NEW CODE, each fixed where a rename was the honest fix: ifMatch (the RFC 9110 header in camelCase) was allowlisted BY EXACT NAME with a reason; requestAppeal was renamed createAppealEscalation, confirmMatch was renamed assessExposure, and the local transport parameter named client was renamed transport, because 'request', 'match' and 'client' are SPEC-000 4 synonyms. PROCESS NOTE, RECORDED BECAUSE IT REPEATED AN EARLIER MISTAKE IN THIS NODE: the ledger row for this part was appended AFTER the commit, because the append was run from the repository root, where scripts/ledger.sh does not exist - the same wrong-directory mistake already recorded once in this node. 2026-09-16T13:06:26Z | forge | EP-005 | MILESTONE_NOTE | CORRECTION TO THE TWO PRECEDING M5 PART-2 ROWS (a factual error I made, corrected rather than left standing). WHAT I CLAIMED: both rows end with a paragraph saying the row was appended AFTER its commit, because 'scripts/ledger.sh: No such file or directory' proved the append had failed. WHAT IS TRUE, measured from this ledger's own timestamps and the git log: the row at 13:05:10 was appended BEFORE commit 44e7c3c - the append succeeded and printed nothing, which is what this script does on success; the 'No such file or directory' error came from a DIFFERENT command in the same chained invocation, the verification step 'sh scripts/ledger.sh tail 1', which I ran with the repository root as its working directory, where scripts/ does not exist. I read an error produced by one command as evidence about another. The row at 13:05:29 is therefore a DUPLICATE of the part-2 record, not a separate event, and the process-defect paragraph in BOTH rows is wrong for part 2. The same paragraph in the PART-1 row (12:37:20) IS accurate: that append really did run from the repository root, really did fail, and was really re-run after its commit. RETRACTION: my report to the user repeated the false version for part 2; it is retracted here and in the next report. THE LESSON, STATED AS A RULE FOR THIS NODE: a claim that a command failed is evidence only when the command's OWN output is read - a chained invocation's stderr belongs to whichever command produced it, and a ledger append's success is confirmed by reading the ledger's tail, never by the absence of an error line. The record for M5 part 2 is otherwise complete and unchanged: nothing about the work itself was misreported, only the ordering of one append.

NEW: src/application/security/authority-service.ts and tests/contract/authority-grant.test.ts (17/17). EXECUTION-TIME VERIFICATION TAKES A READER, NOT A GRANT, AND THAT SHAPE IS THE GUARANTEE: verifyAtExecutionTime(authorityGrantId, now, readGrant) cannot be handed what the caller read earlier in the request, so 'the grant was valid when the request started' is not an argument the function can be given. The suite proves the difference with a TIME-SHIFTED grant - valid at request start, expired at write time - which is impossible to write against an API that accepts a pre-read grant - and with a grant revoked after the earlier read. EACH BINDING RULE IS A SEPARATE FUNCTION BECAUSE EACH ANSWERS A DIFFERENT QUESTION: assertScope (VG-AUTHZ-004), verifyAtExecutionTime (VG-AUTHZ-005), effectiveScope (VG-AUTHZ-007), assertSubjectBinding (VG-AUTHZ-008), plus contest and revoke; folding them together is how the one that reads the grant too early gets lost in a refactor. THE EFFECTIVE SCOPE IS THE INTERSECTION, NOT THE UNION: two grants minted for different purposes do not add up to a third capability nobody granted, and the suite asserts that vg.actions.execute does NOT appear in the effective scope of a wide plus a narrow grant. A SECOND REVOCATION IS REFUSED RATHER THAN IDEMPOTENT, because the second call's timestamp would RE-WRITE history: the first revocation is the fact and a later one would move it; revokeGrant returns a new value and the suite asserts the original is unchanged. THE VOCABULARY CONFLICT IS RESOLVED AS THE PLAN REQUIRES, NOT SILENTLY: SPEC-005 3 names SELF, AGENT, PARENT_GUARDIAN, LEGAL_REPRESENTATIVE while SPEC-003 5.2.1 shows SELF|AGENT|GUARDIAN|DEPENDENT; the SPEC-005 set is the canonical domain enum, an explicit two-way mapping exists, the suite asserts it round-trips, and an UNMAPPED wire value is AUTHORITY_KIND_UNSUPPORTED rather than a default - a daemon that mapped an unknown kind to SELF would silently grant a subject's own authority to an agent's request. MINTING WRITES NOTHING: it validates and returns the grant, so a refusal cannot leave a row behind, and the suite counts rows to show it. EVERY NON-SELF KIND REQUIRES A RECORDED NOTICE ARTEFACT (VG-AUTHZ-014/VG-AUTH-032), a SELF grant requires identity level IAL1 or above, and a zero-scope or already-expired grant is refused. STILL TO DO IN M4: src/application/security/enrollment-controls.ts (velocity limits per tenant/IP class/payment instrument with audit, the same-subject agent conflict VG-AUTHZ-013, and the cooling-off computation from the injected Clock), the repository port and adapter, and tests/contract/enrollment-controls.test.ts. STANDING ACCESS IS NOT A STATE, and the suite asserts the ABSENCE as a property of the declared set rather than as a behaviour: the lifecycle is exactly REQUESTED, ACTIVE, EXPIRED, REVOKED, so a state machine that cannot express 'indefinitely' is what keeps ROLE-2 true after the next edit. THE REQUESTED LIFETIME IS CAPPED RATHER THAN REFUSED - four hours becomes sixty minutes - because a cap is what ROLE-2 states, and a session that silently outlived its request would be the standing access the role prohibits. EXPIRY IS EVALUATED ON EVERY CHECK RATHER THAN BY A SWEEPER: isLive is false exactly at the boundary (60 minutes is over, 59:59 is not), a session past its box is refused even though nothing swept it, and an UNAVAILABLE CLOCK REFUSES because 'I cannot tell whether this session is still live' is not a reason to allow it. A SESSION IS SINGLE-TENANT AND THE REFUSAL SAYS NOTHING ABOUT THE OTHER TENANT: the suite asserts the message does not contain the other tenant's id, because a refusal that confirms the other tenant exists is an enumeration channel. EVERY PII-CLASS READ APPENDS ITS OWN AUDIT EVENT with the field class, and exit appends the exit; a revoked session cannot read afterwards. IMPERSONATION DOES NOT EXIST AS A CAPABILITY RATHER THAN AS A FLAG: diagnosticView has identifierValues typed null and no function returns the subject record, which is how ROLE-3 is enforced by absence. ROLE-4's audit immutability is a function that throws for every role and every operation, so there is no exception to grant. ENUMERATION INHIBITION IS ASSERTED BY EQUALITY: absent and present-but-unauthorised produce the SAME object, and the only distinguishing information is what an AUTHORISED caller receives. PROGRESSIVE LOCKOUT ESCALATES THEN REFUSES, and THE SUITE FOUND A REAL OFF-BY-ONE IN MY OWN LADDER: the first version clamped the index with Math.max(0, n-1), so ZERO failures returned the first delay - a one-second penalty for a request that had not failed - and past the end of the ladder the delay now holds rather than dropping back to zero. 2026-09-16T18:20:09Z | forge | EP-006 | MILESTONE_NOTE | M10 part 1 of 2 - the credential-free security negative cases. NEW: tests/security/negative-cases.test.ts (7/7). THIS SUITE COMPOSES THE MODULES THE EARLIER MILESTONES BUILT, which is the point of a negative-case suite: each module has its own passing suite, and what a reviewer needs is evidence that they compose into the three headline refusals. A REFUSAL IS NOT A FAILURE, AND THE SUITE ASSERTS THE DIFFERENCE: every case counts the effects and the dispatch attempts, so an implementation that refused the caller while still writing would fail here even though its returned code looked right - a revoked grant produces ZERO external actions, a scope violation produces zero, and a refused boundary request dispatches nothing. THE EXECUTION-TIME CASE IS DRIVEN TWICE, once as a revocation after the case was prepared and once as the TIME-SHIFTED grant: the second is the case an API that accepted a pre-read grant could not express, and it is why verifyAtExecutionTime takes a reader rather than a grant. THE SCOPE CASES cover a read-scoped grant that cannot write, a grant bound to one subject that cannot act for another, and the boundary gate refusing both a missing scope and a wrong role. THE CROSS-TENANT CASE ASSERTS THE CLIENT-FACING SHAPE RATHER THAN ONLY THE DECISION: for all seven roles the answer is RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND, the serialised decision contains no TENANT_SCOPE_VIOLATION token and does not echo the other tenant's identifier, which is what SPEC-006 H-9 requires - the body must be byte-identical to a genuinely absent resource, so an internal tenant code must never reach a body. A DEFECT IN MY OWN FIRST VERSION, FOUND BY THE RUNNER RATHER THAN BY REVIEW: I had this suite import the UI's region-state mapping to assert the client half of H-9, and the module path was stale - but the import itself was the bigger problem, since a security suite reaching into the presentation layer couples the node's security evidence to a component that may move, and it did move. The import and that assertion are removed; the client-presentation half is recorded as belonging to the UI suites, and the header says so beside the RLS half. THE RLS HALF IS RECORDED AS BLOCKED RATHER THAN STUBBED: SPEC-005 VG-AUTH-022 requires denial by the service layer AND by row-level security, the second needs a provisioned database and the EP-003 policies, and tests/integration/cross-tenant-both-layers.test.ts is BLOCKED_CREDENTIALS (DATABASE_URL) and BLOCKED_PREREQUISITE (EP-003). STILL TO DO IN M10: tests/integration/authority-at-execution.test.ts, tests/integration/cross-tenant-both-layers.test.ts and tests/integration/evidence-immutability.test.ts - the three real-PostgreSQL proofs the plan lists, which need the database provisioned for the integration stage and are the rows that make VG-AUTH-022 and evidence immutability two-layer claims rather than one-layer ones. Verified: negative-cases 7/7, test-unit 1070/1070, collection guard ok, gate-security ok, reality-gate ok, typecheck ok, format-check ok, copy-lint gate ok, import-boundary ok.

A MISSING CLIENT IS UNKNOWN, NEVER A PASS: a dependency nobody wired is a dependency nobody is checking. tests/observability/readiness-fail-closed.test.ts adds 22 tests: the six keys and their actions; the enum equality with the catalogue; a pass-through evaluation; PER-DEPENDENCY INDUCTION FOR ALL SIX KEYS, each asserting NOT_READY, the dependency named in failedChecks, a classified reason code, and the other five still evaluated; the missing-client case; liveness independent of all six failing at once; the metric labels and the failure counter; the four real client factories discriminating (a role that bypasses RLS fails, a valkey round trip returning the wrong value fails, zero fresh heartbeats fail, a non-HTTPS issuer and an empty key set fail, a 5xx provider response fails); the failure classifier; and the §7.4 step-3 NEGATIVE CASE - a STATIC readiness handler that always answers READY FAILS the assertion that the verdict agrees with the probe results, which is what proves the procedure discriminates a real check from a fabricated one. TWO FINDINGS RECORDED IN THE CODE THEY AFFECT. (1) THE PER-PROBE TIMEOUTS SUM ABOVE THE TOTAL BUDGET: §7.2 declares 300+200+200+400+300+400 = 1800 ms of per-probe hard timeouts AND a 1500 ms total readiness budget, so a run in which several dependencies hang cannot satisfy both however the probes are scheduled; the runner therefore reports budgetExceeded and treats a breach as NOT_READY, because reporting READY after an evaluation that overran the specification would hide it, and a test asserts the 1800 ms sum so the finding cannot be forgotten if the timeouts change. THE job-worker ROW THEREFORE REMAINS EXACTLY AS IT WAS: FAIL in control, induced and remediated, INCONCLUSIVE, with its provisioning attempt log. NO PART OF THE INDUCTION WAS DEMONSTRATED AND NOTHING ABOUT IT IS CLAIMED. WHAT WAS DONE. (1) The stage sources the operator credential file from OUTSIDE the repository (VG-SEC-002), and after sourcing it EXPORTS the names it uses, so the file works even when its lines omit export. (2) MEASURED DEFECT IN MY OWN SOURCING, FIXED: an unquoted value containing a space made the shell try to RUN part of it as a command, AND THE SHELL ERROR MESSAGE PRINTED A FRAGMENT OF THE CREDENTIAL TO THE CONSOLE - the one thing this stage must never do. Sourcing now runs under set +eu with stderr discarded, so a malformed line fails to set its variable, the remaining lines still load, and nothing from the file error text reaches the output; a line that did not load is reported by NAME through the row verdict, never by value. (3) The driver now builds the real provider probe from whichever declared credential is present - Stripe (STRIPE_SECRET_KEY) first, then the search transport (SEARCH_API_KEY, aliased to SERPAPI_API_KEY because that is the operator file name), then Click2Mail (CLICK2MAIL_USERNAME + CLICK2MAIL_PASSWORD, its documented Basic-auth pair rather than a single key) - issuing ONE READ-ONLY request with no body and NO FORM WRITE, which is the declared action of section 7.2 and does not violate section 6.7. (4) THE DECLARED INDUCTION IS IMPLEMENTED: VG_PROVIDER_FORCE_BAD_CREDENTIAL=1 sends the same read-only request with a deliberately wrong credential so the provider returns an auth rejection, which is exactly what section 7.4 step 2 prescribes for this dependency; nothing is stopped, no account is disturbed, and no credential value is ever printed. (5) A DESIGN DEFECT THAT THE LAST WIRING EXPOSED AND THAT IS FIXED: the probes ran SEQUENTIALLY, so once all six were genuinely wired the declared 1500 ms readiness budget became UNSATISFIABLE - measured, keycloak-jwks was refused as TIMEOUT because the budget was already spent, so a HEALTHY dependency was reported broken for no reason but the schedule. They now run CONCURRENTLY, which is the correct reading of a wall-clock budget for a readiness DECISION: a probe does not become unavailable because another probe ran first. A probe that would start past the budget still does not start and is still recorded as a failing required dependency. MEASURED AFTER THE FIX: postgresql, valkey, job-worker, object-store and keycloak-jwks all PASS | FAIL | PASS DEMONSTRATED, and provider-transport reads TIMEOUT | PASS | PASS INCONCLUSIVE - the control run exceeds the declared 400 ms while the induced and remediated runs complete. THE FINDING, MEASURED AND NOT PAPERED OVER: a real unauthenticated round trip to api.stripe.com measured 438 ms from this machine, ABOVE the 400 ms that section 7.2 declares for provider-transport. I did NOT raise the timeout, because that is a specification change and not a gate edit; the honest consequence is that this dependency cannot demonstrate its transition against a real provider from this environment within its declared budget, and the stage therefore still exits non-zero and still prints no sentinel. THE NODE REMAINS OPEN: no NODE_DONE row, no green/EP-008 tag. RELEASE_GATE.json remains INCONCLUSIVE/FORGE_ONLY.
